0x00 — End command / restore CSI

Entry and input. A=command result.

Output, registers and errors. Does not return; stores result at context+68 and reloads CSI.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Releases owner locks/open rows and fixed executive byte locks, but does not CLOSE buffered sequential output. Result zero is not a durability guarantee. See Chapters 26 and 30.

Calling example

xor a
rst 08h
db 00h

The zero in A requests successful command completion. This call does not return; CLOSE any output channels first.

0x01 — Console line input

Entry and input. DE=destination; B=input budget.

Output, registers and errors. A=accepted count; HL/IY restored; BC/DE change.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Budget exhaustion submits without typed CR. Typed CR is stored and followed by another CR; budget exit appends one. CSI underscore deletion depends on context+0A bit 6. B=0 and every editing combination are unspecified.

Calling example

ld de,buffer
ld b,20
rst 08h
db 01h

The caller supplies a buffer larger than the 20-character budget to allow termination bytes. The returned count is in A; do not expect DE or BC to remain unchanged.

0x02 — Display string

Entry and input. DE=string.

Output, registers and errors. DE advances; BC/HL/IY saved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. NUL ends; CR emits newline and ends; LF emits newline and continues. Tab/control behavior follows console context. No caller length is supplied.

Calling example

ld de,message
rst 08h
db 02h

Define message as the bytes for HELLO followed by CR and zero, as in Chapter 18. DE identifies the first byte; the call displays the string.

0x03 — Poll console input

Entry and input. No explicit argument; current console association 8.

Output, registers and errors. Driver/input-state-dependent status; BC/IY preserved on physical status path.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Injected-input state can return NZ directly. Do not interpret carry as a universal input error; see 3E.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 03h

The fragment selects poll console input.

0x04 — Read console character

Entry and input. Current console input.

Output, registers and errors. A=translated character; other complete register effects are not uniformly established.

Structures, restrictions and notes. May block, consume injected input, translate controls and echo through 05. Escape-O suppresses echo without preventing character receipt. CSI/BASIC case and underscore modes differ.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 04h

The fragment selects read console character.

0x05 — Console character output

Entry and input. C=character.

Output, registers and errors. Selected calls preserve BC/DE/HL/IX/IY; AF is not fixed.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Applies suppression and printer-copy policy before 07. Context+0A bit 1 suppresses output. A returned call does not prove physical transmission.

Calling example

ld c,65
rst 08h
db 05h

C contains character code 65, the letter A. This path applies ordinary console policy, including output suppression.

0x06 — Read console character with direct echo

Entry and input. Current console input.

Output, registers and errors. A=character; BC/IY preserved by wrapper.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Uses translated input and echoes values at least 20h through 07, bypassing 05 suppression policy for that echo.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 06h

The fragment selects read console character with direct echo.

0x07 — Terminal-class character output

Entry and input. C=logical terminal character/control.

Output, registers and errors. Selected calls preserve BC/DE/HL/IX/IY; AF changes.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Class translation, cursor tracking and control 10 coordinate consumption apply. Direct 07 bypasses Escape-O. See Chapter 28 for the output layers.

Calling example

ld c,65
rst 08h
db 07h

C contains the letter A. This lower output layer applies terminal-class translation and differs from policy-controlled service 0x05.

0x08 — Printer-association character output

Entry and input. C=printer character; association 0C.

Output, registers and errors. Selected calls preserve BC/DE/HL/IX/IY; AF varies.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Unassigned printer can return 0044 silently. Active spooler collects instead of printing. Common printer control expansion is driver/path dependent.

Calling example

ld c,65
rst 08h
db 08h

The fragment selects printer-association character output.

0x09 — Invalidate cached disk descriptor fields

Entry and input. B=disk association 0.. 7.

Output, registers and errors. BC/DE/HL preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Sets descriptor+14/+15 toFF. Other B values do not mutate. Refresh can add a preliminary header read; this is not a general flush.

Calling example

ld b,0
rst 08h
db 09h

The fragment selects invalidate cached disk descriptor fields.

0x0A — Read logical sector

Entry and input. B=drive association; DE=logical sector; HL=256-byte destination.

Output, registers and errors. Selected normal and ignored-error returns AF0044; C=1; DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Checks existing record spans. Ignore can return invalid data as success; Q exits nonlocally. Invalid associations can abort before I/O. See Chapter 27.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld de,1
ld hl,buffer
rst 08h
db 0Ah

Association 0 selects S. The request reads logical sector 1 into a 256-byte buffer. It does not print that buffer; use the header layout in Appendix E to interpret it.

0x0B — Write logical sector

Entry and input. B=drive; DE=logical sector; HL=256-byte source.

Output, registers and errors. Selected returns AF0044; C=1; DE/HL/IX/IY and source preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Descriptor+13 bit 7 rejects before hardware. Ignoring consumes an unwritten sector as success. Callback clearing protection does not retry an ignored write.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld de,target_sector
ld hl,buffer
rst 08h
db 0Bh

target_sector must identify storage deliberately allocated for this operation. This writes 256 bytes and can destroy metadata if pointed at a header or directory sector.

0x0C — Return initialized kernel signature

Entry and input. Initialized kernel only.

Output, registers and errors. Live routine returns HL=5542h.

Structures, restrictions and notes. The disk entry initially targets initialization 5157; live target 331B differs. Signature meaning and complete register preservation are unknown.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 0Ch

The fragment selects return initialized kernel signature.

0x0D — Create/write directory entry

Entry and input. B=drive; DE=32-byte candidate entry.

Output, registers and errors. Selected creations AF0044; BC/DE/HL/IX/IY and caller bytes preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Normalizes through lookup with public fallback inhibited. Existing/error branch returns AFF/carry by code. Does not establish allocation atomicity or global uniqueness; hidden entries can permit duplicates.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld de,candidate
rst 08h
db 0Dh

The fragment selects create/write directory entry. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x0E — Parse unsigned hexadecimal

Entry and input. DE=uppercase unsigned hexadecimal text.

Output, registers and errors. HL=value/partial overflow result; DE advances; BC restored.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Digits 0–9/A–F, optional H; no sign/space/lowercase. No-digit input can succeed. Overflow consumes its digit and can return a partially shifted HL. Validate syntax and carry independently.

Calling example

ld de,hex_text
rst 08h
db 0Eh

For an elementary input, hex_text can contain ASCII 2A followed by NUL. Check both carry and the advanced DE pointer; success without digit consumption is possible on other inputs.

0x0F — Parse unsigned decimal

Entry and input. DE=unsigned decimal text.

Output, registers and errors. HL=value/partial overflow result; DE advances; BC restored.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Optional D suffix, no sign/space. Empty or D-only input can succeed. Overflow at multiply/add stages returns an intermediate result, not necessarily modulo 65536.

Calling example

ld de,decimal_text
rst 08h
db 0Fh

decimal_text can contain ASCII 123 followed by NUL. Validate trailing text as well as the numeric return.

0x10 — Format byte as hexadecimal

Entry and input. B=byte; DE=destination.

Output, registers and errors. Two uppercase hexadecimal digits; DE+=2; BC/HL unchanged by target; AF changes.

Structures, restrictions and notes. No terminator. Supply two writable bytes.

Calling example

ld b,42
ld de,buffer
rst 08h
db 10h

42 decimal is the byte to format. The caller supplies writable output storage; the call formats rather than printing it.

0x11 — Format unsigned word as decimal

Entry and input. HL=unsigned word; DE=destination.

Output, registers and errors. Writes decimal digits; DE advances; IX restored; AF/alternateAF/BC/HL not generally preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. No leading zeros except single 0 for zero; no terminator. Supply space for five digits.

Calling example

ld hl,123
ld de,buffer
rst 08h
db 11h

123 is the unsigned value to format. buffer must have room for the produced decimal characters and any required termination convention.

0x12 — Console newline

Entry and input. No explicit arguments.

Output, registers and errors. Wrapper preserves AF/BC.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Sends LF then CR through 05. Suppression and printer-copy policy still apply; printer-string newline has another order.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 12h

This requests a console newline using the current console. It requires no separate character buffer.

0x13 — Calibrated busy delay

Entry and input. A=iteration count.

Output, registers and errors. BC preserved; other complete effects unknown.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Calibrated nested busy loop using 0046. A=0 performs 256 iterations. Absolute time unit is unresolved; not a scheduler sleep.

Calling example

ld a,1
rst 08h
db 13h

The fragment selects calibrated busy delay.

0x14 — Find directory entry

Entry and input. DE=writable drive+name 8+type 8; HL=256-byte directory buffer.

Output, registers and errors. Found AF0044, DE=LBA, HL=entry; absent reusable slot AF0100; B=resolved drive. IX/IY preserved in specified matrix; BC changes.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Carry-set returns do not identify a usable slot. Request can be normalized repeatedly. Public fallback, FF drive scan, early 00, deleted-slot memory and short probe cycles are specified in Chapter 26.

Calling example

ld de,filename
ld hl,buffer
rst 08h
db 14h

filename must be writable and contain drive plus two eight-byte fields. The 256-byte buffer receives directory data. Test the result before treating HL as an entry pointer.

0x15 — Get associated device descriptor

Entry and input. B=association.

Output, registers and errors. A/C=physical index; HL=0069h+32*index; DE preserved; carry on unresolved association.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Descriptor fields are a device-family union. Tape utilities add 0F to its driver pointer for a separate private interface.

Calling example

ld b,9
rst 08h
db 15h

The fragment selects get associated device descriptor.

0x16 — Load named executable without entering it

Entry and input. DE=drive+name/type; HL=requested load address in established callers.

Output, registers and errors. Loads A/R without entering; carry marks errors. Selected BASICUSR loads return AF0100 and preserve HL.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Full error/register matrix is incomplete. Do not require A=0 on every successful loader call. See Chapter 18 and Appendix A.

Calling example

ld de,filename
ld hl,load_address
rst 08h
db 16h

The fragment selects load named executable without entering it. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x17 — Printer-association string output

Entry and input. DE=printer string.

Output, registers and errors. DE advances; BC preserved; selected calls also preserve HL/IX/IY.

Structures, restrictions and notes. NUL ends, CR emits CR/LF and ends, LF emits CR/LF and continues. Unassigned printer can succeed without output.

Calling example

ld de,message
rst 08h
db 17h

The fragment selects printer-association string output.

0x18 — Initialize channel name/class

Entry and input. B=channel 0.. 16; DE=18 bytes: drive, name 8, type 8, class.

Output, registers and errors. Copies 18 bytes and clears 14; BC/DE preserved; HL=one past channel. Invalid channel carry.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Does not perform filename normalization. Selected BASIC calls returnAF0040. Internal entry begins context+AC+32*B.

Calling example

ld b,1
ld de,channel_name
rst 08h
db 18h

channel_name contains drive, padded name, padded type and class, totaling 18 bytes. This initializes channel 1; it does not open the file until service 0x20 is called.

0x19 — Convert drive character to association index

Entry and input. B=uppercase drive character.

Output, registers and errors. A=association; carry on invalid/unassigned.

Structures, restrictions and notes. S→0; A–G→1–7 after validation; *→FF. Does not mount media.

Calling example

ld b,53h
rst 08h
db 19h

0x53 is ASCII S. The operation converts that drive character to its disk association index; it does not mount a device.

0x1A — Convert disk association to character

Entry and input. B=disk association.

Output, registers and errors. A=S or A–G; invalid/unassigned gives * and carry; BC/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Inverse display conversion after association resolution.

Calling example

ld b,0
rst 08h
db 1Ah

The fragment selects convert disk association to character.

0x1B — Allocate exact contiguous block count

Entry and input. B=drive; DE=nonzero exact block count.

Output, registers and errors. Success A0/Z/carry 0, HL=first LBA, DE=allocated blocks; failure AFF/NZ/carry 0; association failure carry.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Requires contiguous 1-KiB blocks. C can change; IX/IY preserved in selected tests. Zero and interrupted allocation are not documented.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld de,1
rst 08h
db 1Bh

This requests one contiguous allocation block on S. Keep the returned start address and release only storage you own; allocation changes filesystem state.

0x1C — Free contiguous block range

Entry and input. B=drive; HL=first LBA; DE=block count.

Output, registers and errors. Success A0/Z/carry 0, HL=block-aligned LBA, DE=count.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Clears bitmap and updates free counts under 29A7 lock. No double-free/bounds guarantee. Write-protected header may returnAFF. Normal failed-OPEN rollback is documented.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld hl,allocated_start
ld de,1
rst 08h
db 1Ch

allocated_start must be the start of a one-block run owned by the caller. A guessed address or repeated free can corrupt allocation accounting.

0x1D — Delete named file

Entry and input. DE=drive+name/type.

Output, registers and errors. Missing file can return A0/Z; protected/wrong-owner returnsAFF/carry; DE preserved on ordinary paths.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Marks entryFF before freeing. Deletes S chains by runs. Ignores an exclusive-admission carry in the measured multi-partition path; an exclusive open is not deletion protection.

Calling example

ld de,filename
rst 08h
db 1Dh

The fragment selects delete named file. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x1E — Load and execute command

Entry and input. B=resolved drive; DE=directory entry; HL=CSI argument context.

Output, registers and errors. Loads and transfers control; full initial-register/trampoline contract incomplete.

Structures, restrictions and notes. R enters partition+4CC; A uses directory absolute load address. EX/EXE have documented wrong-token argument behavior. End through 00 in the documented native example.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld de,entry
ld hl,arguments
rst 08h
db 1Eh

The fragment selects load and execute command. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x1F — Rename named file

Entry and input. DE=old descriptor; HL=writable new descriptor.

Output, registers and errors. A08 destination exists, A04 source absent, A0A protection/owner/device refusal; full flags incomplete.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Copies old drive to new descriptor, marks old entryFF, creates renamed metadata. Does not move data; crash atomicity unknown.

Calling example

ld de,old_name
ld hl,new_name
rst 08h
db 1Fh

The fragment selects rename named file. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x20 — Open channel

Entry and input. DE=four-byte request: channel, mode, workspaceLE16; channel initialized by 18.

Output, registers and errors. Selected success A0/Z; BC/HL can change; request mode mutable.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Sequential 88 replaces, 84→8C extends, 90 inputs; direct 58→5A and 59→5B with multiple partitions. Already-open A1; absent 40h, full-probe 20h, protection 0Ah in selected paths. Chapters 17/26 specify failures and buffer obligations.

Calling example

ld de,request
rst 08h
db 20h

request must be a writable four-byte channel/mode/workspace structure, after channel-name initialization. Select the mode intentionally: sequential replacement can delete existing contents during OPEN.

0x21 — Close channel

Entry and input. DE=request as 20.

Output, registers and errors. Selected success A0/Z; BC/HL clobbered; clears channel state.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Flushes sequential tail, slack and metadata in order; clears mode bit 3 on established append paths. Ignored writes can still return success. Releases direct record spans and one file-open row. See Chapter 26.

Calling example

ld de,request
rst 08h
db 21h

Use the request belonging to the open channel. CLOSE completes buffered output handling; it is not interchangeable with generic command termination.

0x22 — Read record from channel

Entry and input. DE=request; HL=destination.

Output, registers and errors. Disk success A0; marker/link EOF A1, possibly after partial copy; DE/HL/IX/IY preserved in selected calls; BC not byte count.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Copies CR record, stops before 1A, no automatic NUL. No visible destination bound. Latches EOF; repeat copies nothing. Device classes dispatch differently.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld hl,buffer
rst 08h
db 22h

The fragment selects read record from channel.

0x23 — Write record to channel

Entry and input. DE=request; HL=record source.

Output, registers and errors. Complete source-length/result/register contract remains incomplete.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Validates open/write state and dispatches disk or device class. Prefer documented counted-output or BASIC examples rather than inventing a terminated-string contract.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld hl,record
rst 08h
db 23h

The fragment selects write record to channel.

0x24 — Format current date

Entry and input. DE=destination.

Output, registers and errors. Formats current date; DE advances; BC/HL preserved after refresh.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Uses BCD state 0013–0015 and 6A; no NUL. Hardware refresh does not independently refresh year.

Calling example

ld de,buffer
rst 08h
db 24h

The fragment selects format current date.

0x25 — Format current time

Entry and input. DE=destination.

Output, registers and errors. Writes exactly eight bytes HH: MM: SS; DE advances 8; BC/HL saved around formatting.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Refreshes time/date through 2C4D/2C57 from BCD globals 001B–001D; no NUL.

Calling example

ld de,buffer
rst 08h
db 25h

The fragment selects format current time.

0x26 — Unsigned division

Entry and input. HL=dividend; DE=divisor.

Output, registers and errors. Unsigned quotient in HL, remainder in DE; BC restored; A=high quotient byte, so Z tests that byte, not the whole quotient; carry on zero divisor.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Zero divisor returns HL0 and original dividend in DE without a trap. Full flags case-specific; seeded BC/IX/IY preserved in native arithmetic controls.

Calling example

ld hl,7
ld de,2
rst 08h
db 26h

The inputs represent 7 divided by 2. Inspect the quotient/remainder contract above; zero divisors require explicit error handling.

0x27 — Unsigned multiplication

Entry and input. HL and DE=unsigned factors.

Output, registers and errors. HL=product if no overflow; BC/DE restored; carry marks overflow. A/Z reflect working multiplier state, not product zero.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Overflow may return an early partial accumulator. 9000h×3 returns 9000h/carry; reversed factors return 3000h/carry. Do not assume low 16 product on failure.

Calling example

ld hl,3
ld de,4
rst 08h
db 27h

The factors are 3 and 4. Test carry before using HL as a valid product; an overflow result is not generally the low word of the mathematical product.

0x28 — Read numbered direct record

Entry and input. DE=request; BC=one-based direct record; HL=record-length destination.

Output, registers and errors. Success A0/Z/carry 0; out of range A80; selected BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Optional inclusive-sector locks remain held after read. Retained same-owner overlap can omit new coverage. Failed range leaves destination unchanged in selected control.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld bc,2
ld hl,buffer
rst 08h
db 28h

The direct file must already be open. BC selects one-based record 2; buffer must accommodate the configured record length.

0x29 — Write numbered direct record

Entry and input. DE=request; BC=one-based direct record; HL=record-length source.

Output, registers and errors. Selected success A0/Z/carry 0 and BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved; closedAFF, protected 0A, range 80 by code/selected controls.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Read/modify/write preserves surrounding sector bytes. Release policy follows request bits. No new explicit span acquisition in measured write-back path.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld bc,2
ld hl,record
rst 08h
db 29h

The direct file must already be open for writing. BC selects record 2 and record supplies its data; protection may be rejected at WRITE rather than OPEN.

0x2A — Parse decimal or H-suffixed hexadecimal

Entry and input. DE=numeric text.

Output, registers and errors. HL parsed value, DE advanced; shares 0E/0F preservation/errors.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Looks ahead through hex characters and selects hex only for uppercase H. FF alone succeeds as decimal zero without consuming; FFH→255; 12AB consumes 12; 12DH is hex012D.

Calling example

ld de,numeric_text
rst 08h
db 2Ah

The fragment selects parse decimal or h-suffixed hexadecimal.

0x2B — Read indexed/keyed record

Entry and input. DE=request; BC=fixed key buffer; HL=data destination.

Output, registers and errors. A0 success; A1/NZ/carry 0 missing with unchanged destination on the stated path; BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Can NUL-pad writable key and mutate channel/workspace. I/K layouts differ; see Appendix E.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld bc,key
ld hl,buffer
rst 08h
db 2Bh

The fragment selects read indexed/keyed record.

0x2C — Read next indexed/keyed record

Entry and input. DE=request; BC=writable key destination; HL=data destination.

Output, registers and errors. Returns next key/data; selected A0 and BC/DE/HL preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. I order follows sorted slot links; K scan differs. Full EOF/error/register mapping incomplete.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld bc,key
ld hl,buffer
rst 08h
db 2Ch

The fragment selects read next indexed/keyed record.

0x2D — Write indexed/keyed record

Entry and input. DE=request; BC=key buffer; HL=fixed record source.

Output, registers and errors. Selected success A0/Z; BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Creates/replaces records and updates index, links/count and workspace. Zero first key byte returns immediately by code. Capacity/error paths remain partial.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld bc,key
ld hl,record
rst 08h
db 2Dh

The fragment selects write indexed/keyed record.

0x2E — Pack current date/time

Entry and input. DE=three-byte destination.

Output, registers and errors. Writes packed current date/time.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Layout is Appendix E's three-byte timestamp. Complete register and malformed-time behavior remains incomplete.

Calling example

ld de,packed_date
rst 08h
db 2Eh

The fragment selects pack current date/time.

0x2F — Resolve volume label or one-character drive

Entry and input. DE=eight-byte padded label.

Output, registers and errors. Label success A=index/Z/carry 0; missing A1/NZ/carry 1; DE/HL/IY preserved on scan; B replaced before BC save.

Structures, restrictions and notes. If byte 1 is space, byte 0 is interpreted as drive character. Otherwise scans associations 0.. 7 and refreshes invalid descriptors.

Calling example

ld de,label
rst 08h
db 2Fh

The fragment selects resolve volume label or one-character drive.

0x30 — Get current partition context

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. IY=word 0033; other target registers unchanged.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Returns current context, not common-global base. GETUSER uses its name field.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 30h

IY receives the current partition context. The fields are laid out in Appendix E; they are not a C structure supplied by an external runtime.

0x31 — Pagination pause

Entry and input. Current terminal/context.

Output, registers and errors. Wrapper preserves AF/BC/IY.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Pagination marker and input wait; accepts space, CR, LF or Ctrl-X. Context flags can suppress pause.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 31h

The fragment selects pagination pause.

0x32 — Read multiple logical sectors

Entry and input. B=drive; C=sector count; DE=start LBA; HL=destination.

Output, registers and errors. Selected C8 calls returnAF0044 and preserve BC/DE/HL/IX/IY.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Shares 0A error policy. C=0, partial failures and atomicity remain unresolved. EXEC usesC2 for 512-byte frames.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld c,2
ld de,1
ld hl,buffer
rst 08h
db 32h

This reads two sectors beginning at logical sector 1 on S into at least 512 bytes of writable storage. Physical requests may include a preliminary descriptor refresh.

0x33 — Write multiple logical sectors

Entry and input. B=drive; C=count; DE=start LBA; HL=source.

Output, registers and errors. Selected C8 calls returnAF0044 and preserve BC/DE/HL/IX/IY/source.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Shares 0B policy; writes may include unchanged sectors. Count 0/partial failure atomicity unknown.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld c,2
ld de,target_sector
ld hl,buffer
rst 08h
db 33h

This writes two sectors from at least 512 bytes of caller data. target_sector must be valid, deliberately selected writable storage; the fragment is not a safe generic disk test.

0x34 — Display string with selected mode flags suppressed

Entry and input. DE=string as 02.

Output, registers and errors. Restores context+0A/+0B and IY.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Temporarily clears 0A bits 1/2 and 0B bit 7 before ordinary string display. Other string effects follow 02.

Calling example

ld de,message
rst 08h
db 34h

The fragment selects display string with selected mode flags suppressed.

0x35 — Schedule, replace or cancel timed callback

Entry and input. HL=six writable bytes followed by callback; DE=delay; A0 normal.

Output, registers and errors. Selected calls preserve AF/BC/DE/HL/IX/IY; return with interrupts enabled.

Structures, restrictions and notes. DE/8 delta ticks; DE0 cancels; expiry clears node and invokes+6 in owner context. A bit 0 head exception is not a general cross-bank cancel. See Chapter 25.

Calling example

xor a
ld hl,timer_node
ld de,80
rst 08h
db 35h

timer_node consists of six writable bytes immediately followed by callback code. Delay 80 is converted to ten eight-millisecond ticks. The callback executes in its owning context.

0x36 — Replace command input and return to CSI

Entry and input. DE=CR-terminated replacement command.

Output, registers and errors. Does not return; cleanup then CSI with result 0.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Copies printable 20–7E, folds lowercase, drops others and sets context+38 bit 1. No visible length guard. Bypasses interactive input budget.

Calling example

ld de,command
rst 08h
db 36h

The fragment selects replace command input and return to csi. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x37 — Get partition memory ceiling

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. HL=context+04 memory ceiling; IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. The ceiling changes as drivers/classes occupy upper memory.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 37h

HL receives the current memory ceiling. Loaded drivers and classes can lower it; do not replace it with a fixed maximum address.

0x38 — Set partition memory ceiling

Entry and input. HL=new memory ceiling.

Output, registers and errors. Stores context+04; IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. No general allocation/validation contract. Changing this pointer alone does not safely allocate memory.

Calling example

ld hl,new_ceiling
rst 08h
db 38h

new_ceiling must fit the partition's valid memory arrangement and retained code. An arbitrary larger value does not allocate more memory.

0x39 — Set context word 28 callback

Entry and input. HL=callback or zero.

Output, registers and errors. Stores context+28; IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Interruption/control callback used by CSI and drivers. SS2's restoration defect belongs to its caller, not this store routine.

Calling example

ld hl,callback
rst 08h
db 39h

The fragment selects set context word28 callback. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x3A — Resolve association

Entry and input. B=association 0.. 23.

Output, registers and errors. A=physical index; valid carry 0/Z0; invalid/unassigned AFF/Z1/carry 1; BC/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Map starts context+7D. Logical association, physical descriptor and partition index are different values.

Calling example

ld b,9
rst 08h
db 3Ah

The fragment selects resolve association.

0x3B — Get associated terminal geometry fields

Entry and input. B=association, normally 8/9.

Output, registers and errors. B = byte at physical-device descriptor +0x02; C = byte at descriptor +0x03; A = byte at descriptor +0x04 (terminal-class number). HL/DE preserved; unresolved association: BC=0x4F17/carry.

Structures, restrictions and notes. L is CSI input budget and an output threshold, P is row cap. Do not assert generic 80×24 from 79/23.

Calling example

ld b,9
rst 08h
db 3Bh

The fragment selects get associated terminal geometry fields.

The offsets above address fields in the physical-device descriptor, not bytes in the terminal-class executable. The descriptor layout is given in Appendix E.

0x3C — Delete indexed/keyed record

Entry and input. DE=request; BC=key.

Output, registers and errors. Deletes found I/K record; absent returns success by code; full register guarantees incomplete.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Marks tombstoneFF, adjusts ordered references/count for I. Not a file deletion.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld bc,key
rst 08h
db 3Ch

The fragment selects delete indexed/keyed record. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x3D — Device initialization dispatch

Entry and input. B=associated character device.

Output, registers and errors. Driver+09 result, not a uniform success status; selected BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Can patch code, register callbacks or exit nonlocally. For floppy drivers+09 means write; do not call it as initialization.

Calling example

ld b,device_association
rst 08h
db 3Dh

The fragment selects device initialization dispatch. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x3E — Device status dispatch

Entry and input. B=association.

Output, registers and errors. Driver+00 result; selected serial calls preserve BC/DE/HL/IX/IY.

Structures, restrictions and notes. ALP Z describes input occupancy, carry DMA readiness; polling can flush output. SS2/printer/tape differ. Unassigned can return 0044, not a distinct error.

Calling example

ld b,8
rst 08h
db 3Eh

The fragment selects device status dispatch.

0x3F — Device input dispatch

Entry and input. B=association.

Output, registers and errors. Character driver's+03; selected serial A=byte/carry 0 with BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. May block/recheck after events. Unassigned returns zero in specified case; printerRET values incidental; tape stub performs no record read; floppy+03 means recalibration request.

Calling example

ld b,8
rst 08h
db 3Fh

The fragment selects device input dispatch.

0x40 — Device output dispatch

Entry and input. B=association; C=character for serial/printer.

Output, registers and errors. Driver/path-dependent AF; selected BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Raw console bypasses class/cursor. Printer policy and spool hook apply by association. Buffered serial return is not transmission completion. Not the disk driver's register ABI.

Calling example

ld b,9
ld c,65
rst 08h
db 40h

The fragment selects device output dispatch.

0x41 — Standard command privilege/serial prologue

Entry and input. Bytes preceding CF41: minimum privilege atP-4; serial atP-3.. P-1.

Output, registers and errors. Pass continues; failure displays 19/20 and terminatesCR252.

Structures, restrictions and notes. First serial byte 0 bypasses binding. Context+0C bit 5 can request version display. Extra utility checks remain separate.

Calling example

db 1,0,0,0
entry: rst 08h
db 41h

Execution must enter at the RST, not at the preceding data. This example requires privilege 1 and uses an all-zero serial field. The prologue is a software guard; it does not elevate privilege.

0x42 — Copy volume label

Entry and input. B=drive; DE=eight writable bytes.

Output, registers and errors. Copies label; DE+=8; BC/HL preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Target lacks explicit unresolved-association carry branch. Validate association before relying on copied bytes.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld de,label
rst 08h
db 42h

The fragment selects copy volume label.

0x43 — Set physical device driver entry

Entry and input. B=physical index 8.. 31; HL=driver entry.

Output, registers and errors. Stores descriptor word; DE preserved; HL ends descriptor+1.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Out-of-range B returns without store. This is physical descriptor installation, not logical association.

Calling example

ld b,physical_index
ld hl,driver_entry
rst 08h
db 43h

The fragment selects set physical device driver entry. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x44 — Terminate/unload associated driver

Entry and input. B=character-device association.

Output, registers and errors. Driver+0C and conditional unload; AF not uniform.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Clears class fields; matching ceiling branch reclaims loaded code. Does not CLOSE application files. DIS1/2 have no fifth entry; disk removal uses another path.

Calling example

ld b,device_association
rst 08h
db 44h

The fragment selects terminate/unload associated driver. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x45 — Poll and consume console-event flag

Entry and input. Current console.

Output, registers and errors. A1/NZ if context+0B bit 5 was set, elseA0/Z; clears bit; IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Calls 03 first. Exact event interpretation outside observed cancellation paths remains incomplete.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 45h

The fragment selects poll and consume console-event flag.

0x46 — Replace command input with continuation ceiling

Entry and input. DE=CR command; HL=continuation ceiling.

Output, registers and errors. Does not ordinarily return; sets ceiling/SP and context+38 bit 5, then 36.

Structures, restrictions and notes. CSI later clears bit 5 and jumps to ceiling. Complete safe caller stack/register recipe unspecified.

Calling example

ld de,command
ld hl,continuation
rst 08h
db 46h

The fragment selects replace command input with continuation ceiling. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x47 — Read byte from memory queue

Entry and input. HL=queue header/payload.

Output, registers and errors. EmptyA0/carry 1 unchanged; dataA/carry 0; selected BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Nonempty path enables interrupts; draining retains previous read index. Eight-bit arithmetic can reach header at capacities 254/255. See Chapter 25.

Calling example

ld hl,queue
rst 08h
db 47h

queue must have the header and storage described in Appendix E. Test carry for empty; a returned zero byte can be valid data.

0x48 — Append byte to memory queue

Entry and input. HL=queue; A=byte.

Output, registers and errors. Ordinary selected calls preserve A/BC/DE/HL/IX/IY; flags change; enables interrupts.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Full loop caches occupancy and can spin even after a callback removes data. Capacities above 128 have demonstrated indexing defects. No safe full error return.

Calling example

ld hl,queue
ld a,65
rst 08h
db 48h

The queue must have free capacity before appending character code 65. A full queue can enter a loop that does not notice ordinary consumer progress.

0x49 — Change console-mode bits

Entry and input. B=set mask; C=clear mask.

Output, registers and errors. A=new context+0A; HL/IY restored; B/C masked in place.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Only bits 2–7 apply; preserves bits 0/1. Uses DI/EI. MENSAJE OFF is instead context+0C bit 7.

Calling example

ld b,set_mask
ld c,clear_mask
rst 08h
db 49h

The fragment selects change console-mode bits. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x4A — Set disk-error callback

Entry and input. HL=disk-error callback or zero.

Output, registers and errors. Selected registration preserves AF/BC/DE/HL/IX/IY.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Callback runs locked before retry decrement: A0 ignore, positive replace error, negative abort. Preserve working registers. Reentrant disk calls unspecified.

Calling example

ld hl,error_callback
rst 08h
db 4Ah

The fragment selects set disk-error callback. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x4B — Initialize or change system volume

Entry and input. B=FF cold initialization, otherwise new system association.

Output, registers and errors. Enters initialization/CSI-loading path; not a returning query.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Locates system components and writes kernel clock state. See Chapter 21.

Calling example

ld b,new_system_association
rst 08h
db 4Bh

The fragment selects initialize or change system volume. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x4C — Scaled busy delay

Entry and input. A low 6=count, high 2=scale 1/10/100/1000.

Output, registers and errors. Low 6 zero returns immediately; BC restored; A0/Z on ordinary return.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Scaled busy delay based on 13. Absolute unit unresolved.

Calling example

ld a,1
rst 08h
db 4Ch

The fragment selects scaled busy delay.

0x4D — Get channel-table entry

Entry and input. B=channel 0.. 16.

Output, registers and errors. HL=context+AC+32*B; invalid carry.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Returns an internal 32-byte channel, not the caller's four-byte request.

Calling example

ld b,1
rst 08h
db 4Dh

The fragment selects get channel-table entry.

0x4E — Dispatch terminal control command

Entry and input. A=control letter.

Output, registers and errors. Path-dependent; can change stack/control flow or not return.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Normalizes case; recognizes Q, D, O, P, I, W, A, B, S,],^, C. Established Q/S/O/I/C behavior is in Chapters 4/14/28; other combinations remain partial.

Calling example

ld a,control_letter
rst 08h
db 4Eh

The fragment selects dispatch terminal control command. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x4F — Yield / schedule

Entry and input. Current partition.

Output, registers and errors. Saves/restores register frame and selects runnable context; interrupt-enable state changes.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Circular first eligible, callback residual preference, byte-quantum anomalies. Single-partition path enables interrupts. See Chapter 25.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 4Fh

The fragment selects yield / schedule.

0x50 — Get global base

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. IY=0.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Common-global base, used by BASIC serial policy. Does not imply writable fields are safe to change.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 50h

The fragment selects get global base.

0x51 — Read global byte 003B

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. A=byte 003B.

Structures, restrictions and notes. A serial-related consumer exists, but a complete independent semantic name is not established. Other register effects follow the short target only.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 51h

The fragment selects read global byte003b.

0x52 — Get current scheduler index

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. A=current zero-based scheduler index 000C.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Not account number, bank number or displayed partition number.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 52h

The fragment selects get current scheduler index.

0x53 — Release channel record locks

Entry and input. DE=request with channel 0.. 16 atbyte 0.

Output, registers and errors. ValidA0/Z; selected BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved; invalidA04/NZ by code.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Releases current-owner/channel record spans. Does not release file-open exclusive admission. Single-partition path skips table operation.

Calling example

ld de,request
rst 08h
db 53h

The fragment selects release channel record locks.

0x54 — Acquire byte lock

Entry and input. HL=one-byte lock.

Output, registers and errors. Selected immediate/delayed calls preserve AF/BC/DE/HL/IX/IY.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Zero→owner=index+1; same owner returns without recursion count; other owner setswaitbit 4/yields/rechecks. IFF preservation/fairness/timeouts unknown.

Calling example

ld hl,lock_byte
rst 08h
db 54h

lock_byte must be a shared byte initialized to zero before use. Acquisition records ownership and can yield while waiting. Repeated acquisition by the owner adds no recursion count.

0x55 — Release byte lock

Entry and input. HL=one-byte lock.

Output, registers and errors. Selected calls preserve AF/BC/DE/HL/IX/IY.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Another owner is ignored, without an error flag. Own release clearsbit 4 in all rows thenlock; wakeup is broadcast and requires recheck.

Calling example

ld hl,lock_byte
rst 08h
db 55h

Release only according to the lock ownership protocol. Woken partitions recheck their own conditions; wakeup does not itself grant a lock.

0x56 — Enter callback in selected partition

Entry and input. A low 7=target index, bit 7=suppressEI; HL=callback in target mapping.

Output, registers and errors. Restores target frame and jumps to callback; callbackRET resumes target.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Masks high state nibble, including STOP. Sender arguments are not restored as target registers. Invalid/nested targets have no general safe contract. See Chapter 25.

Calling example

ld a,target_index
ld hl,callback
rst 08h
db 56h

The fragment selects enter callback in selected partition. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x57 — Get association-map base

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. IY=context+7D; DE preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Points to 24 association bytes. Unassigned isFF.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 57h

The fragment selects get association-map base.

0x58 — Send text to another partition console

Entry and input. A=target index; DE=sender string.

Output, registers and errors. Selected BC/HL/IX/IY preserved; DE advances for send, unchanged for skip; A is not a generic status.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Seven-bit text protocol via recipient callback and 21DD lock. Self/range skips can release an already caller-owned lock; OFF policy is not checked here. See Chapter 29.

Calling example

ld a,target_index
ld de,message
rst 08h
db 58h

The fragment selects send text to another partition console.

0x59 — Acquire fixed global lock

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. Acquire lock 0006 through 54; restores callerHL.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Same ownership/wait semantics and limitations as 54.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 59h

The fragment selects acquire fixed global lock.

0x5A — Release fixed global lock

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. Release lock 0006 through 55; restores callerHL.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Same broadcast/nonowner semantics and limitations as 55.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 5Ah

The fragment selects release fixed global lock.

0x5B — Get sector workspace

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. HL=context+2CC; DE preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Returns 256-byte sector workspace. It can be used internally by other services; avoid unsupported lifetime assumptions.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 5Bh

The fragment selects get sector workspace.

0x5C — Get privilege byte

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. A=context+11 privilege; IY preserved; selected BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. GETPRIV zero-extends intoHL. Getter does not elevate authority.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 5Ch

The result in A is the current privilege. Reading it does not change the account or grant a higher privilege.

0x5D — Compare memory

Entry and input. BC=count; DE/HL=memory ranges.

Output, registers and errors. Compares until difference/exhaustion; pointers/count advance.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Initial BC0 is not established as a safe empty comparison. Full flag/register contract outside comparison use remains incomplete.

Calling example

ld bc,4
ld de,first_bytes
ld hl,second_bytes
rst 08h
db 5Dh

The fragment selects compare memory.

0x5E — Read counted sequential bytes

Entry and input. DE=request; HL=destination; B=count, 0=256.

Output, registers and errors. A0 ordinary, A1 EOF possibly after copying; selected DE/HL/IX/IY preserved, BC changes.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Raw 254-byte payload stream, eager next-sector fetch, no terminator. Exact final-boundary read can copy all bytes and returnEOF. LatchedEOF prevents later copying; see Chapter 26.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld hl,buffer
ld b,4
rst 08h
db 5Eh

The open sequential request supplies four bytes to buffer. A boundary return can indicate EOF even after all requested bytes were copied; inspect the documented status.

0x5F — Write counted sequential bytes

Entry and input. DE=request; HL=source; B=count.

Output, registers and errors. SelectedA0/Z/carry 0; DE/HL/IX/IY restored; BC can change.

Structures, restrictions and notes. One channel-count increment per call. Partial buffer not durable untilCLOSE; B0's 256-loop behavior implementation-defined. Trailing 1A can later be trimmed byEXTEND.BASIC may change request toA8/AC first.

Calling example

ld de,request
ld hl,record
ld b,4
rst 08h
db 5Fh

The open output request accepts four bytes from record. They can remain buffered until explicit CLOSE; this call is not a persistence guarantee.

0x60 — Display numbered system message

Entry and input. HL=one-based message record; B=system drive; DE=eight-byte substitution fields.

Output, registers and errors. BC/DE/HL/IY saved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Reads 64-byte records atsector 25h+(HL-1)//4, offset 64*((HL-1)%4). Global 000E bits 6/7 affect display. Invalid number bounds not checked here.

Calling example

ld hl,1
ld b,0
ld de,substitutions
rst 08h
db 60h

Record 1 is the syntax-error message. substitutions points to the caller's fixed-width substitution area, even when the chosen text needs no substitutions.

0x61 — Display numbered message and end command

Entry and input. As 60, plus A=command result.

Output, registers and errors. Displays message then terminates through 00; no return.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Message number and command result are separate numbers.

Calling example

ld hl,1
ld b,0
ld de,substitutions
ld a,8
rst 08h
db 61h

This displays message 1 and terminates with result 8. It does not return to the instruction after the selector.

0x62 — Load R submodule from compact descriptor

Entry and input. A=format; B=drive; DE=baseLBA; HL=seven-byte module descriptor.

Output, registers and errors. Loads R submodule through constructed metadata; complete return/error matrix incomplete.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Descriptor: loadLE16, imagebytesLE16, storedsectorsbyte, relativeLBALE16. Used by BASIC; do not invent general object-linker behavior.

Calling example

ld a,module_format
ld b,0
ld de,base_sector
ld hl,module_descriptor
rst 08h
db 62h

The fragment selects load r submodule from compact descriptor. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x63 — Allocate a block run with minimum size

Entry and input. B=drive; DE=desired blocks; HL=minimum, both nonzero.

Output, registers and errors. SuccessA0/Z/carry 0, HL=firstLBA, DE=count; no runAFF/NZ/carry 0; unresolved associationcarry.

Structures, restrictions and notes. First desired-size run, else largest qualifying run with later ties. BC changes; selectedIX/IY preserved. Shares locked bitmap updater with 1B/1C.

Calling example

ld b,0
ld de,4
ld hl,1
rst 08h
db 63h

The desired run is four blocks and the minimum acceptable run is one. The allocator can return a smaller qualifying run; use its returned count, not the requested count.

0x64 — Look up an abbreviable keyword table

Entry and input. DE=token; HL=keyword table.

Output, registers and errors. MatchA0/Z, HL=result; absentA1/NZ; DE restored; BC clobbered.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Record: minimum-prefixbyte, keyword with finalbit 7, resultLE16; zero minimum ends table. A–Z/0–9/$ accepted; caller folds case. First qualifying match wins.

Calling example

ld de,token
ld hl,keywords
rst 08h
db 64h

The fragment selects look up an abbreviable keyword table.

0x65 — Get account number

Entry and input. No explicit input.

Output, registers and errors. A=context+1A account number; IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. GETID zero-extends intoHL. Public 0, temporaryFF and partition lock ownership are distinct concepts.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 65h

A receives the account number. This is different from service 0x52, which returns the scheduler index.

0x66 — Filter device input for escape controls

Entry and input. A=input character; IY=device descriptor.

Output, registers and errors. Enabled path restores BC/HL; callback can change other state; carry means consumed in established paths.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Descriptor+05 bit 7 enables, bit 6 tracks escape; optional+16 callback. Calls 4E after escape/case handling. Not a universal raw character filter.

Calling example

ld a,input_byte
ld iy,device_descriptor
rst 08h
db 66h

The fragment selects filter device input for escape controls. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x67 — Register indexed callback

Entry and input. A=index 02.. 20h; DE=callback.

Output, registers and errors. Stores owner and pointer and pointer at 23FF+3*A; selected AF/BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Invalid range returns without store by code. Does not itself DI; enabled-caller atomicity unknown. DMA owner rows need not invoke their stored callback.

Calling example

ld a,callback_index
ld de,callback
rst 08h
db 67h

The fragment selects register indexed callback. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x68 — Copy from selected record bank

Entry and input. A=record index; HL=source there; DE=destination in caller; BC=positive count.

Output, registers and errors. BC0; HL/DE advance; A/IX/IY preserved; returnsDI.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Cross-bank preservesAF, uses common stack; same-bankLDIR changesF and overlaps propagate forward. No pointer/index/zero-count validation. Current identity pointers unchanged.

Calling example

ld a,target_index
ld hl,source_address
ld de,destination
ld bc,16
rst 08h
db 68h

target_index selects a scheduler record, not a physical bank number. Sixteen bytes are copied into the caller's destination. Account for the disabled-interrupt return.

0x69 — Copy to selected record bank

Entry and input. A=record index; HL=caller source; DE=destination there; BC=positive count.

Output, registers and errors. As 68.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Direction reverses 68. Record index is not bank. BC0 could iterate 65536; this and arbitrary overlap with control structures are not supported recipes.

Calling example

ld a,target_index
ld hl,source
ld de,destination_address
ld bc,16
rst 08h
db 69h

The source belongs to the caller and the destination to the selected record's mapping. The count is sixteen; zero is not an empty-copy convention.

0x6A — Format supplied BCD date

Entry and input. C=BCD month, H=BCD day, L=BCD year; DE=output.

Output, registers and errors. DE advances 8 normally or 9 with bothformatbits; BC/HL/IY preserved.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Context+0C bit 4 choosesMM/DD/YY, defaultDD-MM-YY; bit 2 aloneDDMMMYY plus space. Spanish months. No NUL; invalidBCD/month unchecked.

Calling example

ld c,01h
ld h,01h
ld l,90h
ld de,buffer
rst 08h
db 6Ah

The supplied BCD date is 01/01/90. buffer must be writable and large enough for the selected date format; this formats data rather than changing the clock.

0x6B — Wait for device event or interrupt

Entry and input. Current device/event condition managed by caller.

Output, registers and errors. SelectedAF/BC/DE/HL/IX/IY preserved; returns with interrupts enabled.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Single partitionEI/HALT; multiple setwaitbit 5 and schedule. Wakeup may carry no input. Always recheck condition.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 6Bh

Wait only inside a loop that rechecks the relevant condition. A wakeup need not mean a character is available.

0x6C — Find named resident component

Entry and input. DE=eight readable exact key bytes.

Output, registers and errors. FoundAF0044/HL=address; absentAF0101/HL unchanged; BC/DE/IX/IY preserved on the stated paths.

Structures, restrictions and notes. Ten-byte rows from 04BC, stopfirst 00/space; no numeric bound. No normalization, size, bank or owner field. Exact SPOOLER-space resolves 39C2.

Calling example

ld de,component_key
rst 08h
db 6Ch

component_key must contain exactly eight readable bytes, for example SPOOLER followed by one space. Check success before using HL as the returned address.

0x6D — Write current global time through RTC callback

Entry and input. Current global BCD time 001B–001D; callback 0062 installed.

Output, registers and errors. Wrapper preserves AF/BC/DE/HL.

Structures, restrictions and notes. WRITES RTC through 2E88 to 84/83/82 and enables interrupts. It is not a read-only clock query.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 6Dh

The fragment selects write current global time through rtc callback. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

0x6E — Write current global date through RTC callback

Entry and input. Current global month/day 0013/0014; callback 0064 installed.

Output, registers and errors. Wrapper preserves AF/BC/DE/HL.

Structures, restrictions and notes. WRITES RTC through 2E98 to 87/86; does not write year 0015. Callback configuration can differ on another machine.

Calling example

rst 08h
db 6Eh

The fragment selects write current global date through rtc callback. This is an internal or configuration operation, not an ordinary application setup shortcut.

Chapter 33 — Diagnostics and messages

A message record number, a bracketed number inside its text, a BASIC runtime error and a CSI command result are separate namespaces. Record 30 itself contains the bracketed number 29. Do not renumber it to make the table regular. Some records are prompts or successful status messages rather than errors.

SISTEMA.MENSERR contains 200 fixed 64-byte records: 141 have nonblank text, four contain only CR, and 55 are zero-filled. The table preserves all 145 nonzero records; omitted records are zero-filled. {1} and similar fields are substitutions supplied by callers. A literal vertical bar is preserved as text. Control bytes are shown as \xNN. No trailing byte has been reinterpreted as an English punctuation mark.

The subsystem column is an editorial aid based on the text and identified callers. For records with an unspecified caller, the text gives a meaning without promising a universal corrective action. Chapter 13 gives the diagnostic workflow.

Diagnose common workflow failures

The following table relates displayed diagnostics to specific operations. Its CR column gives the CSI command result where observed; it is not the message-record number in the catalogue below. Some commands display message numbers only when configured to do so.

Displayed text Operation and result Corrective action
Esta cuenta esta siendo utilizada por la particion 1 CONECTAR; CR = 10; partition number varies End the existing connection under that account number before retrying. The password has not yet been checked. See password changes.
No se ha cambiado ningun fichero CAMPRO; CR = 12 Check selection and connect as the current file owner with sufficient privilege. SISTEMA does not automatically select another owner's private files.
La cuenta sistema no puede compartir ficheros COMPART; CR = 12 Use a non-SISTEMA file-owning account with privilege 3 or higher.
Error sintactico ATRIB attribute-only example; CR = 8 Supply one filename for an attribute change; reserve the two-name form for renaming.
Falta tipo del fichero AYUDA BORRAR fallback; CR = 8 Use the BORRAR reference. AYUDA has invoked the utility with AYUD instead of displaying its topic.
COMU1 no esta asociado BSC AYUDA; CR = 16 Use AYUDA BSC for central help. The utility itself requires compatible COMU1 setup.
Opcion no valida BASIC with COMMA or COMA; CR = 8 These compiler options are rejected in this version. Use dot-decimal source.
FALTAN PARENTESIS Four-argument DEL$ example; BASIC correction editing Use the demonstrated three-argument form in Appendix D. Do not assume that a CSI prompt follows this BASIC diagnostic.
NECESITA EXPRESION STRING NBR with numeric argument; BASIC correction editing Supply a string expression as in NBR. The returned value's meaning remains unspecified.
[27] Fich. Cerrado LINE(1), PAGE(1) or POS(1) with no channel open The bare example lacks an open-channel context. Acceptance of its syntax does not establish a useful query result.

Command results and BASIC errors

Value or text Established meaning and action
CSI CR0 Normal reported completion in selected paths; does not prove flush, delivery or physical I/O
CSI CR8 Several syntax/device/file refusals; use accompanying text
CSI CR12 Several search/storage/spool refusals; not a unique kernel error
CSI CR252 Standard command privilege/serial prologue denial
CSI CR253 BASIC runtime error path, including policy or computed-branch failure
CSI CR254 Active interruption/disk-abort paths; also CLAVIN startup failure. The code alone does not identify the cause. Protected CLOSE can instead report 0
BASIC [30] Fich. no Esta Missing or unreachable file, including early 00-hidden entries
BASIC [32] Directori.Llen Directory lookup exhaustion; other catalogue slots can remain unused
BASIC error 33 Established OPEN/WRITE protection refusal; distinguish operation and owner
BASIC [40] Programa no esta en posicion 4CED Observed unsuccessful RUN; verify BASICCOM and runtime path rather than treating 4CED as a fixed universal address
BASIC [41] Format Fich Invalid Invalid record type; ignored I/O/latched EOF can leave stale destination bytes that BASIC interprets as a type
Computed ON error 0x14 Selector 0 on the specified branch path; ordinary result 253

Native checker diagnostics

Diagnostic text or parameterized form Meaning Action
***** Fin de fichero incorrecto Traversed final S sector disagrees with directory end Back up the chain and entry; F does not guarantee reconstruction of the final sector
La longitud del fichero no coincide con el directorio Rounded chain extent differs from declared allocation Keep both interpretations and prior metadata
Sector <n> ocupado pero no pertenece a ningun fichero The numbered sector is allocated but has no owner in the checker scan Preserve candidate data before F frees the bits

The checker does not necessarily report a stale sequential count, duplicate identity or application-level EOF gap. Its silence is not a complete filesystem certification.

Disk-driver status

The specified driver vocabulary includes 01 not ready (no prep.), 02 write-protected, 03 uninitialized, 04 transfer error, 07 sector/ID failure, 08 track failure and 09 head failure. Exact meanings vary with driver translation and not every physical failure has a complete defined contract. R retries; M invokes the driver's recalibration/reset slot and retries; I ignores; Q aborts nonlocally. The installed WD M slot is RET. Ignoring does not reconstruct data. See Chapter 27 for callback policy and the protected-CLOSE result-zero exception.

Chapter 34 — Devices and system limits

Logical names designate uses within a partition. Physical names select installed definitions, which may describe optional hardware not fitted to the machine. The presence of a name is therefore weaker evidence than a successful device operation.

Logical association index

Index (hex) Name or role
00 S, system disk
01–07 A–G, other disks
08 Console input
09 Console output
0A–0B Remaining slots not fully classified here
0C–0F IMPR1–IMPR4
10 COMU1 in the established caller
11–13 Further communication slots; full usage not independently exercised
14 TAPE1 in DIS10 initialization
15–17 Remaining slots; complete active-device mapping unknown

The association byte is zero-based physical descriptor index, withFF unassigned. The displayed definition number is index+1. Thus installed DISCO1/device 3 resolves to descriptor index 2, at 0x0069+2*32=0x00A9. ASOCIAR manipulates logical-to-physical assignment; ASIGNAR assigns application channels. A filename uses name.type:drive, with S and A–G interpreted through the current partition.

Physical names

Name Definition number Driver/class and availability
FLOPPY1, FLOPPY2 1,2 DIS1/2 with separate controller paths; Chapter 11 covers access and the FLOPPY2 format; fitted mechanism and bootstrap compatibility require matching hardware
DISCO1–DISCO6 3–8 Disk definitions; S association is configuration-dependent
TAP 10 DIS10; controller/physical tape operation unresolved
IP, CDC 11,12 Printer drivers; printer-driver definitions
PAN9, PAN10; IS9, IS10 16,17 DIS16/17 on the SS2 SCC; 128-byte input rings and polled output; hardware and matching terminal framing required
PAN1–PAN4 18–21 ALP consoles; four established serial ports
IMP 22 DIS22 serial printer interface using the SCN2651 register layout and modem readiness; physical printer model unspecified
PAN5–PAN8; IS5–IS8 23–26 Optional second ALP; optional hardware
COM1–COM4 27–30 Definitions; corresponding complete operational drivers not established

IS2/IS3/IS4 share the corresponding PAN2/PAN3/PAN4 driver files. Their names do not imply that every default mode or simultaneous alias configuration has been specified. Appendix B gives exact driver sizes, entry offsets, ports and callback indices.

Canonical limits

Quantity Established value Scope
CPU address space 65,536 bytes Z80 visible window
Implemented RAM 256 KiB:16 common +5×48 banked Example five-bank configuration
Example physical disk 830×5×32×256 =33,996,800 bytes Example geometry; verify fitted device
Logical volume sectors 62,048 Example volume geometry
Allocation blocks 15,512 of 1,024 bytes Example volume geometry
Sector/payload 256 physical/logical;254 S payload Established formats
Catalogue slots 1,208 for 151 directory sectors 151×8; probe defect can lower reachable capacity
Filename/type Eight bytes each Space-padded; normalization restrictions apply
Timestamp years 1977–1992 Four-bit year offset; not full hardware RTC range
Associations 24 per partition Context layout
Application channel indices 0–16 17 entries; user ASIGNAR help exposes 1–16
Scheduler rows 16 Table and public numbering; sixteen active partitions not proven
Account name Eight characters in CUENTA Use an initial letter followed by letters/digits for ordinary new names
Account password Eight-character field in CUENTA Input removes spaces; comma rejected; Chapter 10 explains blank and # answers
Account numbers CUENTA allocates new distinct numbers through 250; CAMCU help permits 0–254; FF is special Different command ranges; maximum simultaneous accounts/sessions not established
Privilege values 0–5 ordinary documented/specified range Additional internal 6/FF checks do not establish user levels
Default terminal fields L79/P23 Input budget and cursor thresholds, not generic display dimensions
EXEC arguments 16×8 bytes 17th supplied field excluded in specified case
EXEC variables 16 name 8/value 8 cells per frame Layout; all exhaustion behavior not established
EXEC frame 512 bytes Installed work files offer four frames each
Record-lock table 64 usable +terminator Code/layout; full-table behavior not induced
File-open table 129 physical 4-byte rows including needed terminator storage Effective saturation behavior unknown
I/K record field R=(packed&511)+1 Representational 1–512; creation/runtime limits may be narrower
I/K key field K=(packed>>9)+1 Representational 1–128; complete extreme-value behavior unknown
BASIC decimal 13 significant decimal digits in specified representation Arithmetic rounding/range not fully established
Maximum program size Unknown as one constant Depends on partition, ceiling, drivers/classes and runtime
General simultaneous open-file maximum Unknown Channel count and shared table capacity are distinct
Spool queue 64 stored records; producer appears to wrap 1–63 Code-derived effective capacity, native saturation untested
Safe queue arithmetic range 1–128 under valid serialized state Derived avoidance of specific defects, not an advertised OS maximum

Capacity fields must not be promoted to guaranteed usable maxima without checking sentinel rows, reserved records and algorithms. Conversely, a small example does not establish the system's maximum.

Chapter 35 — Command spelling and language reference

The system has several command languages. CSI selects a system command; each utility then interprets its own arguments. EDITAR, EXEC, CONSULTA and BASIC each recognize a separate vocabulary. This chapter supplies spelling and lookup details after the working introductions in Chapters 5, 7 and 8. BASIC's language reference remains in Appendix D.

Full command names and permitted abbreviations

Use the full names in the Command column when following procedures. Most commands accept every prefix between their listed minimum and full name. The Accepted spellings column lists the exact alternatives so that exceptions are visible. Recognition depends on abbreviation being enabled and the standard table being selected. A recognized name does not create an executable that is absent.

BASIC is an exception: B selects BASIC, BA selects BACKUP, and BAS through BASIC select BASIC. EXEC has a separate argument-transfer limitation: EX and EXE are recognized but can run a command file named EX or EXE instead of the requested file. Always enter EXEC in full. DESCARGAR is a nine-character table name constrained by an eight-character command field; it has no available matching executable.

Command Accepted standard spellings Notes

The following executable names have no standard abbreviation. They are already the full names of their commands, even where their spelling is compact:

ARCHDIR, BSC, CAMCU, CEDIT, CIFRA, CLAVIN, CONSULTA, COPFICH, CORLUZ, DIRIBM, DUPLICAR, ENVIAR, ENVIARA, ESPIAR, FISDEF, HASH, INICDISC, INICIBM, INITTAPE, LOGDEF, MODIMPR, O8PRECOM, ORGDIR, PANTALLA, RECIBIR, RECIBIRA, SERIAL, SORT, TECLAS, TERMINAL, TITULO, UNOSEC, VERFORM.

LCOMPART has a help topic but no matching executable or standard table entry. CARGAR, CLASIF and DESCARGAR retain table entries but have no available executable or EXEC file; DESCARGAR is looked up as DESCARGA. CLASIF does not redirect to SORT. Conversely, CLAVIN is installed but returns CR 254 during startup in tested invocations. Their Chapter 31 entries distinguish recognized names, installed files and usable commands.

Enable or disable abbreviation

At CSI, enter:

PONER ABREV ON

This enables command-prefix expansion. To require complete command names, enter:

PONER ABREV OFF

Exact filenames and exact configured synonyms still work. These settings do not disable a utility's option abbreviations.

To select the built-in abbreviation table, enter:

PONER SINONIM (S)

To disable that table, enter:

PONER SINONIM (N)

Disabling the table does not rename command files. To display its entries, enter:

ESTADO SINONIMO

This lists the standard names and their advertised minima. There is a limitation when a user synonym file is selected: CSI reads type SINONIMO, but this ESTADO program looks for SINONIM. The listing can therefore end with file-not-found/CR 12 even when the custom synonyms work. Keep the working definition file's SINONIMO type.

User synonyms and filename precedence

A synonym supplies another name for a command. For example, a definition file named SHORT.SINONIMO can contain this line:

ESTADO SHOW 2

This is file content, not a CSI command: ESTADO is the real name, SHOW the synonym, and 2 the minimum synonym length. Create the file using the text-editing procedure in Chapter 7. Then select it at CSI:

PONER SINONIM SHORT

With abbreviation enabled, SHOW, SHO and SH select ESTADO unless an actual file takes precedence. With abbreviation disabled, the exact synonym SHOW still works. To cancel the custom definitions, enter:

PONER SINONIM (C)

Custom synonyms are considered before standard expansion. Within each searched disk association, CSI tries the original token's COMANDO file, then its EXEC file if implicit execution is enabled, before trying the expanded names. Only then does it continue to another association. Thus an actual ASO.COMANDO or enabled ASO.EXEC can take precedence over expansion to ASOCIAR. The full EXEC command follows its own earlier interpreter path. See Chapter 30 for loading.

Utility options: full keywords and shorter forms

The tables below record accepted spellings, not complete operating procedures. Use each command's Chapter 31 entry for purpose, arguments, precautions and examples. Recognition of an option does not establish that the requested hardware operation can complete. Full keywords are shown first; accepted abbreviations are alternatives rather than corrected names.

Most tabulated options accept the intermediate prefixes shown. Selection follows each utility's table order, so overlapping prefixes can select different operations. For example, COPIFI PA selects PANT; use PACK to request packing. SPOOLER SAL selects a different operation from SALVAR, although S and SA select SALVAR. These exceptions are recorded explicitly below.

A full keyword means the literal word recognized by that program. ESTADO uses HISTOR and INIREL, while PONER uses HISTORIA and INIRELOJ. They are separate interfaces. COPIFI's lowercase selector is LMINUSC; MINUSC and MINUSCU are not aliases. COPIFI's append keyword is literally A@ADIR, with an at sign. Its short form A has the same append function.

Options with character-based parsing

Some utilities inspect individual letters instead of using the tables below. Do not expand these forms into invented words.

Command Form Interpretation and restrictions
ASOCIAR L79, P23, C2, B9600 Width, page, class and speed forms have attached numbers. L 79 and LINEA79 are not substitutes for L79.
ASOCIAR LFnn, FFnn Device-specific delay fields; separate from width and page dimensions.
ASOCIAR W8 Accepted word-size form; W7 is rejected.
ASOCIAR PE, PO, PP, ALF, PUBLIC Parity, page/line handling and public association flags; see the command's device-specific options.
ASOCIAR ENAB1–ENAB4, O, H, R, STP, STS Individually parsed device settings; arguments and effects depend on the device.
ASOCIAR SY, SD, WA Recognized branches whose complete operator contracts remain unspecified.
ATRIB LE Read protection. L is also accepted; LEER is not. NOL requires privilege 255 and is not an unrestricted inverse operation.
ATRIB E/NOE, B/NOB Write- and deletion-protection controls.
BORRAR PANT/NOPANT, CONSUL/NOCONSUL Display and consultation controls. Exact-name deletion can proceed without a consultation prompt.
LISTAR PA followed by page number Page selection; other P-leading forms select printer output, with a final digit selecting printer 1–4.
LISDIR FN, FT, FD, FO, FI, AP, NOS, NOH Individual field, output and listing selectors; see LISDIR rather than applying CSI's abbreviation rules.

Use balanced option parentheses in examples. The tokenizer also accepts a carriage return as the end of options, which explains command files that omit the closing parenthesis. This does not make spaces optional inside numeric arguments: ASOCIAR uses L79, whereas CREAR uses REGISTRO 128.

PONER settings

A setting follows PONER and takes its own arguments. For example:

PONER IMPEXEC ON

This enables implicit EXEC-file lookup. It does not change the spelling rules inside an EXEC file.
